to the measure space
\left(\left[0,+\infty\right),\mathscr{B}\left(\left[0,+\infty\right)\right)\right)
through the function
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
3
). Denote the resulting measure in
\left(\left[0,+\infty\right),\mathscr{B}\left(\left[0,+\infty\right)\right)\right)
by
P
.
More specifically, for all
\mathcal{A}\in\mathscr{B}\left(\left[0,+\infty\right)\right)
, it holds that
P\left(\mathcal{A}\right)=Q\left(\left\{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}:\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\in\mathcal{A}\right\}\right)
.
Hence, the function
K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
satisfies for all
t\in\left\{\nu\in\mathds{R}:K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\nu)<+\infty\right\}
,

\displaystyle K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(t\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log\left(\int\exp\left(t\;\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)

(353)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log\left(\int\exp\left(t\,w\right)\mathrm{d}P(w)\right),

(354)

where the equality (
354
) follows from
[
59
, Theorem
1.6.12
]
.
Denote by
\phi
the Laplace transform of the measure
P
. That is, for all
t\in\left\{x\in\mathds{R}:K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(x)<+\infty\right\}
,

\phi(t)=\int\exp\left(t\,v\right)\mathrm{d}P(v).

(355)

Hence,
\phi(t)=\exp\left(K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(t\right)\right)
.
From
[
74
, Theorem
1a
(page 439)]
, it follows that the function
\phi
has derivatives of all orders in
\left\{x\in\mathds{R}:K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(x)<+\infty\right\}
, and thus, so does the function
K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
in the interior of
\left\{x\in\mathds{R}:K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(x)<+\infty\right\}
. This completes the proof.

Appendix L
Proof of Lemma
16

Let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathds{R}^{2}
, with
\gamma_{1}\neq\gamma_{2}
and
\alpha\in[0,1]
be fixed. Assume that
K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\gamma_{1}\right)<+\infty
and
K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\gamma_{2}\right)<+\infty
.
Then, for all
\alpha\in(0,1)
, the following holds

\displaystyle\alpha K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\gamma_{1}\right)+(1-\alpha)K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\gamma_{2}\right)

(357)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha\log\left(\int\exp\left(\gamma_{1}\,\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)

\displaystyle+(1-\alpha)\log\left(\int\exp\left(\gamma_{2}\,\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log\left(\left(\int\exp\left(\gamma_{1}\,\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)^{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle+\log\left(\left(\int\exp\left(\gamma_{2}\,\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)^{(1-\alpha)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log\bigg(\left(\int\exp\left(\gamma_{1}\,\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)^{\alpha}

(358)

\displaystyle\left(\int\exp\left(\gamma_{2}\,\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)^{(1-\alpha)}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log\Bigg(\left(\int\exp\left(\gamma_{1}\alpha\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)^{p}\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\left(\int\exp\left(\gamma_{2}(1-\alpha)\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)^{q}\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\Bigg)

(359)

\displaystyle\geqslant

\displaystyle\log\left(\int\exp\left(\gamma_{1}\alpha\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\exp\left(\gamma_{2}(1-\alpha)\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)

(360)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log\left(\int\exp\bigg(\left(\gamma_{1}\alpha+\gamma_{2}(1-\alpha)\right)\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)

(361)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\gamma_{1}\alpha+\gamma_{2}(1-\alpha)\right),

(362)

where
the inequality in (
359
) follows with
\alpha\triangleq\frac{1}{p}
and
1-\alpha\triangleq\frac{1}{q}
;
the inequality in (
360
) follows from Hölder’s inequality. Hence, equality in (
360
) holds if and only if there exist two constants
\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{2}
, not simultaneously equal to zero, such that the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\left\{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}:\beta_{1}\exp\left(\gamma_{1}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)=\beta_{2}\exp\left(\gamma_{2}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\right\}

(363)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}:\exp\left(\left(\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}\right)\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)=\frac{\beta_{2}}{\beta_{1}}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}:\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)=\frac{\log\frac{\beta_{2}}{\beta_{1}}}{\left(\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}\right)}\right\},

(364)

satisfies
Q\left(\mathcal{A}\right)=1
.
That is, strict inequality in (
360
) holds if and only if the function
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
is separable with respect to the
\sigma
-finite measure
Q
.
When
\alpha=0
or
\alpha=1
, the proof is trivial.
This completes the proof.

Appendix M
Proof of Lemma
17

For all
s\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
, with
\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
23
), the equality in (
97
) implies the following,

\displaystyle K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)

(365)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\log\left(\int\exp\left(t\,\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\Biggr|_{t=-\frac{1}{s}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\exp\left(t\,\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)}{\int\exp\left(t\,\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{v})}\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})\Biggr|_{t=-\frac{1}{s}}

(366)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{s}\,\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)}{\int\exp\left(-\frac{1}{s}\,\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{v})}\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})

(367)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(-K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)\right)\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\exp\left(-\,\frac{1}{s}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})

(368)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\exp\left(-K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)-\frac{1}{s}\,\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})

(369)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,s)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}),

(370)

where
the equality in (
366
) holds from the dominated convergence theorem
[
59
, Theorem
1.6.9
]
;
the equality in (
368
) follows from (
22
); and
the equality in (
370
) follows from (
25
).

For all
s\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
, with
\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
23
), the equalities in (
97
) and (
369
) imply that

\displaystyle K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)

(371)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\exp\left(-K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(t\right)+t\,\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})\Biggr|_{t=-\frac{1}{s}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\left(-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(t\right)+\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\exp\left(-K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(t\right)+t\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})\Biggr|_{t=-\frac{1}{s}}

(373)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\left(-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)+\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\exp\left(-K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)-\frac{1}{s}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\left(-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)+\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,s)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(374)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,s)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle+\int\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,s)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(375)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)\right)^{2}+\int\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,s)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(376)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,s)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right),

(377)

where
the equality in (
373
) follows from the dominated convergence theorem
[
59
, Theorem
1.6.9
]
;
the equality in (
374
) is due to a change of measure through the Radon-Nikodym derivative in (
25
); and
the equality in (
376
) follows from (
370
).

For all
s\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
, with
\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
23
), the equalities in (
97
) and (
376
) imply that

\displaystyle K^{(3)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)

(378)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\displaystyle\int\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,-\frac{1}{t}\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-\left(K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(t\right)\right)^{2}\right)\Biggr|_{t=-\frac{1}{s}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Bigg(\displaystyle\int\bigg(\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)^{2}\exp\left(-K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(t\right)+t\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}Q\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle-\left(K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(t\right)\right)^{2}\Bigg)\Biggr|_{t=-\frac{1}{s}}

(379)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\exp\left(-K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(t\right)+t\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\Biggr|_{t=-\frac{1}{s}}\right)\mathrm{d}Q\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle-2K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(t\right)K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(t\right)\Biggr|_{t=-\frac{1}{s}}

(380)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)^{2}

(381)

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(t\right)\right)\exp\left(-K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(t\right)+t\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\Biggr|_{t=-\frac{1}{s}}\mathrm{d}Q\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle-2K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(t\right)K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(t\right)\Biggr|_{t=-\frac{1}{s}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)^{2}\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\exp\left(-K_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)-\frac{1}{s}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle-2K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)

(382)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)^{2}\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,s\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle-2K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)

(384)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)^{3}\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,s\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)\int\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,s\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle-2K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)^{3}\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,s\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)\left(K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)+\left(K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle-2K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)

(385)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)^{3}\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,s\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)^{3}

(387)

\displaystyle-3K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{s}\right)\right)^{3}\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,s\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right),

(388)

where
the equality in (
379
) follows from (
25
);
and the equality in (
380
) follows from the dominated convergence theorem
[
59
, Theorem
1.6.9
]
;
the equality in (
384
) follows from (
25
); and
the equality in (
385
) follows from (
376
).

This completes the proof.

Appendix N
Proof of Theorem
5

The proof is based on the analysis of the derivative of
K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)
with respect to
\lambda
in
\mathrm{int}\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
. This is due to Corollary
2
.
For instance, note that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\lambda}\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\lambda}K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)

(389)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)

(390)

\displaystyle\geqslant

\displaystyle 0,

(391)

where the equality in (
390
) follows from Lemma
17
.
The inequality in (
391
) implies that the expected empirical risk
\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)=K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)
in (
106
) is nondecreasing with respect to
\lambda
.
The rest of the proof consists in showing that for all
\alpha\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
, the function
K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
97
) satisfies
K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)>0
if and only if the function
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
3
) is separable.
For doing so, a handful of preliminary results are described in the following subsection. The proof of Theorem
5
resumes in Subsection
N-B

N-A

Preliminaries

Given a positive real
\lambda\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
, with
\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
23
), consider a partition of
\mathcal{M}
formed by the sets
\mathcal{R}_{0}(\lambda)
,
\mathcal{R}_{1}(\lambda)
and
\mathcal{R}_{2}(\lambda)
, such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{0}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\left\{\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathcal{M}:\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)=\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\right\},

(392a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\left\{\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathcal{M}:\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)<\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\right\},\mbox{ and }

(392b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\left\{\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathcal{M}:\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)>\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\right\},

(392c)

where the functional
\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
is in (
18
) and the probability measure
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
is in (
25
).
The sets in (
392
) exhibit several properties that are central for proving the main results of this section.

Lemma 26

The probability measure
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
25
), satisfies

P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{1}(\lambda)\right)>0,

(393)

if and only if

P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{2}(\lambda)\right)>0,

(394)

where the sets
\mathcal{R}_{1}(\cdot)
and
\mathcal{R}_{2}(\cdot)
are in (
392b
) and (
392c
), respectively.

Proof:

The proof is divided into two parts. In the first part, given a real
\alpha\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
, it is proven that if the set
\mathcal{R}_{1}\left(\alpha\right)
is nonnegligible with respect to
P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}~
, then the set
\mathcal{R}_{2}\left(\alpha\right)
is nonnegligible with respect to
P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}~
.
The second part proves the converse.

The first part is proved by contradiction. Assume that set
\mathcal{R}_{2}\left(\alpha\right)
is negligible with respect to
P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}~
. Hence, from Lemma
17
, it holds that

\displaystyle K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)

(395)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{0}(\alpha)}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)+\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{1}(\alpha)}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{2}(\alpha)}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{0}(\alpha)}\hskip-10.76385pt\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)+\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{1}(\alpha)}\hskip-10.76385pt\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)

(396)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{0}(\alpha)\right)

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{1}(\alpha)}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)

(397)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{0}(\alpha)\right)

\displaystyle+K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{1}(\alpha)\right)

(398)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)\big(P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{0}(\alpha)\right)+P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{1}(\alpha)\right)\big)

(399)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right),

(400)

which is a contradiction.

The second part of the proof follows the same arguments as in the first part. Assume that the set
\mathcal{R}_{1}\left(\alpha\right)
is negligible with respect to
P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}~
. Hence, from Lemma
17
, it holds that

\displaystyle K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)

(401)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{0}(\alpha)}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)+\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{1}(\alpha)}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{2}(\alpha)}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{0}(\alpha)}\hskip-12.91663pt\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)+\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{2}(\alpha)}\hskip-12.91663pt\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)

(402)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{0}(\alpha)\right)

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{2}(\alpha)}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)

(403)

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{0}(\alpha)\right)

\displaystyle+K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{2}(\alpha)\right)

(404)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)\left(P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{0}(\alpha)\right)+P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{2}(\alpha)\right)\right)

(405)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right),

(406)

which is also a contradiction. This completes the proof.
∎

A more general result can be immediately obtained by combining Lemma
4
and Lemma
26
.

Lemma 27

For all
\alpha\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
, with
\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
23
), the measure
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
25
), satisfies

P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{1}(\alpha)\right)>0,

(407)

if and only if

P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{2}(\alpha)\right)>0,

(408)

where the sets
\mathcal{R}_{1}(\alpha)
and
\mathcal{R}_{2}(\alpha)
are in (
392b
) and (
392c
), respectively.

N-B

The proof

The rest of the proof of Theorem
5
is divided into two parts.
In the first part, it is shown that if for all
\alpha\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
,
K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)>0
, then the function
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
3
) is separable.
The second part of the proof, consists in showing that if the function
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
is separable, then, for all
\alpha\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
,
K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)>0
.

The first part is as follows.
From Lemma
17
, it holds that for all
\alpha\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
,

\displaystyle K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)

(410)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{0}\left(\alpha\right)}\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{1}\left(\alpha\right)}\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{2}\left(\alpha\right)}\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right),

where the sets
\mathcal{R}_{0}(\alpha)
,
\mathcal{R}_{1}(\alpha)
, and
\mathcal{R}_{2}(\alpha)
are respectively defined in (
392
).
Hence,

\displaystyle K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)

(411)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{1}\left(\alpha\right)}\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{2}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right).

(412)

Under the assumption that for all
\alpha\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
the function
K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
97
) satisfies
K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)>0
, it follows that at least one of the following claims is true:

(a)

P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{1}(\alpha)\right)>0
; and

(b)

P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{2}(\alpha)\right)>0
.

Nonetheless, from Lemma
26
, it follows that both claims
(a)
and
(b)
hold simultaneously.
Hence, the sets
\mathcal{R}_{1}(\alpha)
and
\mathcal{R}_{2}(\alpha)
are both nonnegligible with respect to
P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}~
and moreover, it holds that for all
\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1},\boldsymbol{\nu}_{2}\right)\in\mathcal{R}_{1}(\alpha)\times\mathcal{R}_{2}(\alpha)
,

\displaystyle+\infty>\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle>K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)>

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{2}\right),

(413)

where
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}\right)<+\infty
follows from the fact that
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\left\{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}:\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)=+\infty\right\}\right)=0
(Lemma
2
).
This proves that under the assumption that for all
\alpha\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
,
K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)>0
, the function
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
3
) is separable with respect to
P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
. From Lemma
14
, it holds that the function
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
is separable with respect to
Q
. This completes the first part of the proof.

The second part of the proof is simpler. Assume that the empirical risk function
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
3
) is separable with respect to
P^{(Q,\alpha)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
. That is, for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
, there exist a positive real
c_{\gamma}>0
; and two subsets
\mathcal{A}(\gamma)
and
\mathcal{B}(\gamma)
of
\mathcal{M}
that are nonnegligible with respect to
P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
25
) and verify that for all
(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1},\boldsymbol{\nu}_{2})\in\mathcal{A}(\gamma)\times\mathcal{B}(\gamma)
,

\displaystyle+\infty>\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle>c_{\gamma}>

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{2}\right).

(414)

From Lemma
17
, it holds that

\displaystyle K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)

(415)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{A}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(416)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(417)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}\setminus\left(\mathcal{A}(\gamma)\cup\mathcal{B}(\gamma)\right)}\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\,\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(418)

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle 0,

(419)

where the inequality (
419
) follows from the following facts.
First, if
c_{\gamma}<K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)
, with
c_{\gamma}
in (
414
), then for all
\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathcal{B}(\gamma)
, it holds that
K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)>c_{\gamma}>\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)
, and thus,

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\left(c_{\gamma}-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\right)^{2},

(420)

which implies,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(421)

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\left(c_{\gamma}-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\right)^{2}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{B}\left(\gamma\right)\right)

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle 0.

(422)

Second, if
c_{\gamma}\geqslant K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)
then for all
\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathcal{A}(\gamma)
, it holds that
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)>c_{\gamma}\geqslant K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)
, and thus,

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\left(c_{\gamma}-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\right)^{2},

(423)

which implies,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{A}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(424)

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\left(c_{\gamma}-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\right)^{2}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{A}\left(\gamma\right)\right)

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle 0.

(425)

Hence, under the assumption that the empirical risk function
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
3
) is separable, it holds that for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
,

K^{(2)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)>0
.
This completes the proof.

Appendix O
Proof of Lemma
19

Consider the partition of the set
\mathcal{M}
formed by the sets
\mathcal{A}_{0}
,
\mathcal{A}_{1}
, and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
in (
318
).
From (
98
), for all
\lambda\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
, with
\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
23
), it holds that,

\displaystyle K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)

(427)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\int_{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\int_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

(428)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}})+\int_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

(429)

\displaystyle\geqslant

\displaystyle\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}})+\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\mathcal{A}_{2})

(430)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star},

(431)

where
the equality in (
428
) follows by noticing that
Q\left(\mathcal{A}_{1}\right)=0
, which implies that
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\mathcal{A}_{1})

=

0
(Lemma
3
);
the equality in (
429
) follows from noticing that
\mathcal{A}_{0}=\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
, with
\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
38
); and
the equality in (
430
) follows from (
318c
).
This completes the proof.

Appendix P
Proof of Theorem
6

From (
429
) in the proof of Lemma
19
, it holds that

\displaystyle\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0^{+}}K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)

(432)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0^{+}}\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}})+\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0^{+}}\int_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\hskip-6.45831pt\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0^{+}}\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}})

\displaystyle+\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0^{+}}\int_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})

(433)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0^{+}}\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}})

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}Q(\boldsymbol{\theta})

(434)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0^{+}}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}})

(435)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star},

(436)

where,
the equality in (
434
) follows from noticing two facts:

(a)
For all
\lambda\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
, the Randon-Nikodym derivative
\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}
is positive and finite (Lemma
2
); and

(b)
For all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{A}_{2}
, it holds that
\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)=0
(Lemma
6
).
Hence, the dominated convergence theorem
[
59
, Theorem
1.6.9
]
holds.
The inequality in (
435
) follows from Lemma
7
.
This completes the proof.

Appendix Q
Proof of Theorem
9

From Theorem
5
, it follows that for all
(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\times\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
with
\lambda_{1}>\lambda_{2}
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{(Q,\lambda_{1})}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}Q\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda_{2}\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}Q\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right),

which implies the following inclusions:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}(\lambda_{2})

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}(\lambda_{1}),\mbox{ and }

(437a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}(\lambda_{1})

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}(\lambda_{2}),

(437b)

with the sets
\mathcal{R}_{1}(\cdot)
and
\mathcal{R}_{2}(\cdot)
in (
392
).
From (
168
), it holds that for all
i\in\{1,2\}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda_{i})=\mathcal{R}_{2}(\lambda_{i})^{\sf{c}},

(438)

where the complement is with respect to
\mathcal{M}
.
Thus, the inclusion in (
437b
) and the equality in (
438
) yields,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda_{1})\supseteq\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda_{2}).

(439)

The inclusion
\mathcal{M}\supseteq\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda_{1})
follows from (
168
).
Alternatively, the inclusion
\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda_{2})\supseteq\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}
, follows from Lemma
19
and from observing that for all
\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda_{2}\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant

\displaystyle\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}=\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right),

(440)

which implies that
\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda_{2})
.
This completes the proof of (
172
).

The proof of (
173
) is as follows. From the intermediate value theorem
[
70
, Theorem
4.23
]
and the assumption that the empirical risk function
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
3
) is continuous on
\mathcal{M}
, it follows that for all
\lambda\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
, there always exists a model
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}
, such that

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)=\displaystyle\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right),

(441)

which implies that
\mathcal{R}_{0}\left(\lambda\right)
is not empty, and as a consequence,
\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda\right)=\mathcal{R}_{0}\left(\lambda\right)\cup\mathcal{R}_{1}\left(\lambda\right)
is not empty.
Hence, for all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{R}_{0}\left(\lambda_{1}\right)
it holds that
\boldsymbol{\theta}\notin\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)
. This proves that the elements of
\mathcal{R}_{0}\left(\lambda_{1}\right)
are in
\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{1}\right)
but not in
\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)
.
This, together with (
439
), verifies that

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{2}\right).

(442)

The strict inclusion
\mathcal{M}\supset\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda_{1})
is proved by contradiction. Assume that there exists a
\lambda\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
such that
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda)
. Then,
\mathcal{R}_{2}\left(\lambda\right)=\emptyset
and thus,
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{2}\left(\lambda\right)\right)=0
, which together with Lemma
26
, implies that
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{1}\left(\lambda\right)\right)=0
and consequently,

P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{0}\left(\lambda\right)\right)=1.

(443)

This contradicts the assumption that the function
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
is separable (Definition
5
). Hence,
\mathcal{M}\supset\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda_{1})
.

Finally, the strict inclusion
\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda_{2})\supset\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}
is proved by contradiction.
Assume that there exists a
\lambda\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
such that
\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}=\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda)
. That is,

\displaystyle\left\{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}:\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\leqslant\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}\right\}

(444)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda)

(445)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}:\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\leqslant K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\,\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)\right\}.

(446)

Hence, three cases might arise:

(a)
there exists a
\lambda\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
, such that
\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}<K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)
and it holds that

\left\{\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathcal{M}:\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}<\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\leqslant K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)\right\}=\emptyset;

(b)
there exists a
\lambda\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
, such that
\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}>K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)
and it holds that

\left\{\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathcal{M}:K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)<\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\leqslant\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}\right\}=\emptyset;

or

(c)
there exists a
\lambda\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
, such that
\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}=K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)
.

The cases
(a)
and
(b)
are absurd. Hence, the proof is complete only by considering the case
(c)
. In the case
(c)
, it holds that,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\left(\lambda\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathcal{M}:\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)<\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}\right\},

(447)

and from the definition of
\delta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{\star}
in (
37
), it holds that

P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{1}\left(\lambda\right)\right)=0.

(448)

From Lemma
26
and (
448
), it follows that,

P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{2}\left(\lambda\right)\right)=0.

(449)

Finally, by noticing that

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{0}\left(\lambda\right)\right)+P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{1}\left(\lambda\right)\right)

(450)

\displaystyle+P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{2}\left(\lambda\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{0}\left(\lambda\right)\right),

(451)

reveals a contradiction to the assumption that the function
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
is separable with respect to
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
(and thus, separable with respect to
Q
by Lemma
14
).
This completes the proof of (
173
).

Appendix R
Proof of Theorem
10

The proof of (
174
) is based on the analysis of the derivative of
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
with respect to
\lambda
, for some fixed set
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathscr{B}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
.
More specifically, given a
\gamma\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
, it holds that

\displaystyle P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{A}}\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}Q\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right),

(452)

and from the fundamental theorem of calculus
[
70
, Theorem
6.21
]
, it follows that for all
(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\times\mathcal{K}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
with
\lambda_{1}>\lambda_{2}
,

\displaystyle P^{\left(Q,\lambda_{1}\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)-P^{\left(Q,\lambda_{2}\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)

(453)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda_{2}}^{\lambda_{1}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\gamma}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\mathrm{d}\gamma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda_{2}}^{\lambda_{1}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\gamma}\int_{\mathcal{A}}\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}Q\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}\gamma

(454)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda_{2}}^{\lambda_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{A}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\gamma}\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}Q\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}\gamma,

(455)

where
the equality in (
454
) follows from (
452
); and
the equality in (
455
) holds from Lemma
2
and the dominated convergence theorem
[
59
, Theorem
1.6.9
]
.

For all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathrm{supp}\,Q
, the following holds,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\lambda}\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(456)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\lambda}\frac{\exp\left(-\frac{\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)}{\lambda}\right)}{\displaystyle\int\exp\left(-\frac{\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)}{\lambda}\right)\mathrm{d}Q\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)}{\lambda}\right)}{\displaystyle\int\exp\left(-\frac{\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)}{\lambda}\right)\mathrm{d}Q\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)}{\lambda}\right)\displaystyle\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)}{\lambda}\right)\mathrm{d}Q\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)}{\left(\displaystyle\int\exp\left(-\frac{\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)}{\lambda}\right)\mathrm{d}Q\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\right)^{2}}

(457)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\displaystyle\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}Q\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)

(458)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-\displaystyle\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\right).

(459)

Plugging (
459
) into (
455
) yields,

\displaystyle P^{\left(Q,\lambda_{1}\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)-P^{\left(Q,\lambda_{2}\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)

(460)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda_{2}}^{\lambda_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{A}}\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)-\displaystyle\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}Q\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}\gamma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda_{2}}^{\lambda_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{A}}\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)-\displaystyle\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}\gamma.

Note that for all
\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)
, it holds that for all

,

\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)-\displaystyle\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\leqslant 0,

(462)

and thus,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)}\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)-\displaystyle\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle 0.

The equalities in (
) and (
), with
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda\right)
, imply that

\displaystyle P^{\left(Q,\lambda_{1}\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)\right)-P^{\left(Q,\lambda_{2}\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)\right)

(464)

The inequality

The proof of strict inequality in (

) is divided into two parts.
The first part shows that if for all pairs

\displaystyle P^{\left(Q,\lambda_{1}\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda_{2}))<P^{\left(Q,\lambda_{2}\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda_{2})),

The first part is as follows. In the proof of Theorem

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda_{2}}^{\lambda_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)}\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\,\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\gamma\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}\gamma.

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle>

From the arguments above, it has been proved that given a pair

The second part of the proof is under the assumption that the empirical risk function

\displaystyle>c_{\gamma}>

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{2}\right).

In the proof of Theorem

, cf. (
), it has been proved that given a pair
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\alpha_{2}}^{\alpha_{1}}\hskip-4.30554pt\int_{\mathcal{A}}\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\right)\mathrm{d}\lambda

Appendix S
Proof of Lemma
25

The proof is based on the following two observations. First, note that
\left(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)\right)^{\textsf{c}}

=

\mathcal{R}_{2}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)
, with the set
\mathcal{R}_{2}\left(\cdot\right)
defined in (
392c
).
Second, note that

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)\cup\left(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{1}\right)\cap\mathcal{R}_{2}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)\right),

(488)

and the fact that the sets
\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)
and
\left(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{1}\right)\cap\mathcal{R}_{2}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)\right)
are disjoint.
Hence, for all
i\in\{1,2\}
,

\displaystyle P^{(\lambda_{i})}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda_{1}))

(489)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P^{(\lambda_{i})}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\bigg(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)\cup\left(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{1}\right)\cap\mathcal{R}_{2}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P^{(\lambda_{i})}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\bigg(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle+P^{(\lambda_{i})}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\bigg(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{1}\right)\cap\mathcal{R}_{2}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)\bigg)

(490)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P^{(\lambda_{i})}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\bigg(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)\bigg),

(491)

where the equality in (
490
) follows from Lemma
3
and the equality in (
175
).

Finally, under the assumption that the empirical function
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
3
) is separable, it holds from Theorem
10
that

\displaystyle P^{\left(Q,\lambda_{1}\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda_{2}))<P^{\left(Q,\lambda_{2}\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda_{2})).

(492)

Plugging (
491
) into (
492
), with
i=1
, yields,

\displaystyle P^{\left(Q,\lambda_{1}\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda_{1}))<P^{\left(Q,\lambda_{2}\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\mathcal{N}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}(\lambda_{2})),

(493)

and this completes the proof.

Appendix T
Proof of Theorem
15

Consider the following lemma.

Lemma 28

Given two probability measures
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
over
\left(\mathcal{M},\mathscr{B}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\right)
, with
P_{2}
absolutely continuous with respect to
P_{1}
, the following holds for all
\boldsymbol{z}\in\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{2}\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{1}\right)

(494)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\inf_{t<0}\left(\frac{D\left(P_{2}\|P_{1}\right)+\log\left(\int\exp\left(t\,\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{1}\right)\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{1}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)}{t}\right),

where the function
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
and the functional
\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
are defined in (
3
) and in (
18
), respectively.

Proof:

From
[
57
, Corollary
4.15
, Page 100]
, it follows that the probability measures
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
in
\left(\mathcal{M},\mathscr{B}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\right)
satisfy the following equality:

\displaystyle D\left(P_{2}\|P_{1}\right)=\sup_{f}\int f\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-\log\int\exp\left(f\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right),

(495)

where the supremum is over the space of all measurable functions
f
with respect to
\left(\mathcal{M},\mathscr{B}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\right)
and
\left(\mathds{R},\mathscr{B}\left(\mathds{R}\right)\right)
, such that
\int\exp\left(f\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)<\infty
.
Hence, for all
\boldsymbol{z}\in\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}
and for all
t\in\left(-\infty,0\right)
, it follows that the empirical risk function
\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
3
) satisfies that

\displaystyle D\left(P_{2}\|P_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant

\displaystyle\int t\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-\log\int\exp\left(t\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant

\displaystyle\int t\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle-\log\int\exp\left(t\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)+t\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{1}\right)-t\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{1}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int t\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-t\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{1}\right)

\displaystyle-\log\int\exp\left(t\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-t\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{1}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{2}\right)-t\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{1}\right)-\log\int\exp\left(t\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-t\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{1}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right),

which leads to

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{2}\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{1}\right)

(496)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\frac{D\left(P_{2}\|P_{1}\right)+\log\int\exp\left(t\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{1}\right)\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)}{t}.

Given that
t
can be chosen arbitrarily in
\left(-\infty,0\right)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{2}\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\inf_{t\in\left(-\infty,0\right)}\frac{D\left(P_{2}\|P_{1}\right)+\log\int\exp\left(t\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{1}\right)\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)}{t},

which completes the proof.
∎

From Lemma
28
, it holds that the probability measure
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
in (
25
), satisfies for all
P\in\triangle_{Q}\left(\mathcal{M},\mathscr{B}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\right)
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\leqslant\inf_{t\in\left(-\infty,0\right)}\Bigg(\frac{D\left(P\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)}{t}

\displaystyle+\frac{\log\left(\int\exp\left(t\,\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)}{t}\Bigg),

(497)

where the function
K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
is defined in (
98
) and satisfies (
106
).
Moreover, for all
t\in\left(-\infty,0\right)
,

\displaystyle\log\left(\int\exp\left(t\,\left(\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)-K^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)

(498)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log\left(\int\exp\left(t\,\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)-tK^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{\boldsymbol{z},Q,\lambda}(t)-tK^{(1)}_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)

(499)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\beta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{2},

(500)

where
the equality in (
499
) follows from (
138
);
the inequality in (
500
) follows from Theorem
7
; and the constant
\beta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}
is defined in (
161
).

Plugging (
500
) into (
497
) yields for all
t\in\left(-\infty,0\right)
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)

(501)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\inf_{t\in\left(-\infty,0\right)}\frac{D\left(P\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\beta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}}{t}.

Let the
c\in\mathds{R}
be defined as follows:

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right).

(502)

Hence, from (
501
), it follows that for all
t\in\left(-\infty,0\right)
,

\displaystyle c\,t-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\beta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}\leqslant D\left(P\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right).

(503)

The rest of the proof consists in finding an explicit expression for the absolute value of
c
in (
503
).
To this aim, consider the function
\phi:\mathds{R}\rightarrow\mathds{R}
such that

\phi(\alpha)=\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{2}\beta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{2},

(504)

and note that
\phi
is a positive and strictly convex function with
\phi(0)=0
.
Let the Legendre-Fenchel transform of
\phi
be the function
\phi^{*}:\mathds{R}\rightarrow\mathds{R}
, and thus for all
x\in\mathds{R}
,

\phi^{*}(x)=\max_{t\in\left(-\infty,0\right)}xt-\phi(t).

(505)

In particular, note that

\phi^{*}(c)\leqslant D\left(P\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right).

(506)

Note that for all
x\in\mathds{R}
and for all
t\in\left(-\infty,0\right)
, the function
\phi^{\star}
in (
505
) satisfies

x\,t-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\beta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}\leqslant\phi^{\star}(x)=x\alpha^{\star}(x)-\phi\left(\alpha^{\star}(x)\right),

(507)

where the term
\alpha^{\star}(x)
represents the unique solution in
\alpha
within the interval
\left(-\infty,0\right)
to

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\alpha}\left(x\alpha-\phi\left(\alpha\right)\right)=x-\alpha\beta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}=0.

(508)

That is,

\displaystyle\alpha^{\star}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{x}{\beta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}}.

(509)

Plugging (
509
) into (
507
) yields,

\displaystyle\phi^{\star}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{x^{2}}{2\beta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}}.

(510)

Hence, from (
506
) and (
507
), given
c
in (
502
) for all
t\in\left(-\infty,0\right)
,

\displaystyle c\,t-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\beta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}\leqslant\phi^{\star}(c)\leqslant D\left(P\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right),

(511)

and thus,

\frac{c^{2}}{2\beta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}}\leqslant D\left(P\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right).

(512)

This implies that

c\leqslant\sqrt{2\beta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}D\left(P\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)}

(513)

and

c\geqslant-\sqrt{2\beta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}D\left(P\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)},

(514)

which leads to

\displaystyle\left|\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\mathrm{d}P(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\int\mathsf{L}_{\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right|

(515)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\sqrt{2\beta_{Q,\boldsymbol{z}}^{2}D\left(P\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)},

and completes the proof.

Appendix U
Proof of Theorem
17

Under the condition that
\lambda\in\mathcal{K}_{Q,P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}}
, from Theorem
14
and Definition
8
, it follows that the generalization error
\mathsf{G}_{Q,\lambda}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
8
) satisfies

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}_{Q,\lambda}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\int\Big(D\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\|Q\right)+D\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\right)

(516)

\displaystyle-D\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|Q\right)\Big)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{\nu}),

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\Bigg(\int D\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})

\displaystyle+\int D\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})\Bigg),

(517)

where the equality in (
517
) follows from the fact that

\displaystyle\int\Big(D\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\|Q\right)-D\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|Q\right)\Big)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})

(520)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int D\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\|Q\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})-D\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|Q\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\left(\int\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})

\displaystyle-D\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|Q\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\left(\int\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})

\displaystyle-\int\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\left(\int\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})

\displaystyle-\int\left(\int\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})

(521)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\Bigg(\int\Bigg(\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)

\displaystyle+\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}Q}{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\Bigg)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\Bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})

(522)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\Bigg(\int\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}}{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\Bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})

(523)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int D\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{\nu}).

(524)

The equality in (
521
) follows from (
207
); and
the equality in (
522
) follows from the fact that the measures
Q
and
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}
, with
\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathrm{supp}\,P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
, are mutually absolutely continuous (Lemma
3
).
This completes the proof.
